4. **ADRIAN'S OWN TENURE IS SEVENTY-SIX ON THE FIFTY-FIFTH MORNING AND EIGHTY-FIVE ON THE SIXTY-FOURTH, ONE A MORNING, AND THE INTERMEDIATE MORNINGS ARE EIGHTY ON THE FIFTY-NINTH AND EIGHTY-ONE ON THE SIXTIETH. THESE TWO WERE PRINTED WRONG AND HAVE BEEN REPAIRED; SEE ITEM 14.** Seventy was said on the fifty-fourth morning off the boy's own page; seventy-six is seventy plus six mornings the boy had been in the yard and not on the boards; eighty-five was read off nothing at all by the man of fifty-six at about four on the last morning of this block with nobody having asked him to. **Fifty is not struck. Fifty is what was said on the thirty-ninth morning and it was in about nineteen mouths and no book.** Nothing in this block evened any of them up.
5. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR HAS ASKED ONE QUESTION MORE AND IT IS THE FIRST HE HAS EVER ASKED.** *Is it going to end.* Adrian answered *I don't know* and the man said *Aye.* **He had four hundred and nine nights of never being asked anything at the end of the last block; he has four hundred and nineteen at the end of this one and has now been asked two questions in his life, the first by the man who digs loam on the forty-sixth morning and the second by himself.** He still goes out at about half past four and comes back at first light, is paid nothing, and has no line on any page. **He is on a page with nothing ruled on it in a clerk's own book, against his express wish, and nobody in that district has told him, and that thread is now two blocks old and still open.**
6. **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE IS ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN AND IS STILL ON NO PAGE.** He dictated the fifth line at about twenty-two on an afternoon fifteen years ago and has been at the end of that table for the whole of the fifteen. **In this block he confirmed that the row with *the man* in it is his own and that he wrote it through the boy about two years ago; he asked for it to be struck out, was refused, asked for it to be kept, and had it read out in a yard; it was entered nowhere. He still refuses to be entered in the column and is still unpaid and still not the bearer.** He is the man who sent the block's page up nothing, because he is the man who told Adrian a hearing is not a question.
7. **THE FIFTH LINE IS A STANDING OFFER OF LABOUR, WITHOUT END AND WITHOUT PAY, FIFTEEN YEARS OLD, AND IT HAS NOT BEEN ENDED AND WAS NOT ENDED IN THIS BLOCK.** The man it is about was asked to give it an end on the fifty-second morning of the last block and said no in a margin. **He was not asked again in this block and the argument was not advanced and the line is still on that book working exactly as it was written.**
8. **THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD HAS ONE NAME IN IT, ADRIAN VALE, ENTERED ON THE THIRTY-NINTH MORNING AFTER THE THIRTIETH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AT THE WORD OF A BOY OF SEVENTEEN.** The clerk was asked, in a yard, on the sixtieth morning, whether the man who reads four figures off the wall would give his name, and he said YES ON A CONDITION: a rule about who may be entered, made out loud in a yard in about nineteen people's hearing, before any person is refused, and not made afterwards, because a rule written after a refusal is a rule about that person. **HE IS NOT IN THE COLUMN AND THE COLUMN HAS ONE NAME.** The woman who keeps a scale is still not in it and still does not want to be and still has not been named. Orren Pike has still not been appointed to it and was not appointed to it. **The heading still says *whoever*.**
9. **THE FOUNDER'S CLAUSE IS ON THE PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN VOLUME 16 AND NOTHING ABOUT IT IS OWNED BY THIS DISTRICT.** It exists; it is held; it is held by a person; it has no end on it; Iven Tallow wrote it in about nine months; the Custodian is one person and the clause does not say one man and does not say named in advance; only the holder can rewrite it. **THE NAME OF THE BUILDING IS NOT ENTERED IN THE CLERK'S BOOK AND THE LOCATION IS NOT PRINTED IN HER BOOK.** She told him what she would need — one page she owns, in her own hand, that is not a fair copy — and he said he would send it in three or four days, and it has not come.
10. **THE COMMON MEASURE IS ON THE PAGE, IN IVEN TALLOW'S MOUTH, AT THE FOOT OF A LOW WALL ON THE FIFTY-NINTH MORNING.** It was going to put the essential rights of about four thousand rooms into one open settlement so that none of them could be owned by a market, a ward, or him. It needed two independent witnesses who had understood the terms and had not been given them by anybody, and a term; he wrote both safeguards in himself in about the ninth month. The close came in the sixth week of the last year of the old counting with about nine hundred people in a hall and about four hundred of them frightened of what they had read, and he could not find two people he trusted more than himself and did not go looking long. He became the Custodian and left the obligation open until there was somebody better; there has not been one in seventeen years. It sat open about eleven weeks with him in the gap where the witnesses should have been, and the rights went over on the morning they were supposed to be held, and nothing was holding them. **He says this is the Long Fracture and not a machine and not a judgement. He also named one thing the gap does not excuse: in the ninth year he put his name on the roll of a ward market when nobody asked and nobody could have stopped him, and about four hundred people went without bread in the winter after that.**
11. **THE WAY A CLAUSE WITH NO END ON IT ENDS IS ON THE PAGE AND IS UNPAID.** A closing that names the override, states the remedy that replaces it, and is accepted by at least two fresh independent witnesses who understood the terms and were not given them by anybody. **This district does not have two.** Iven Tallow's own words for it: *that is two people who have never witnessed a term in their lives, and a witness who does not know what the term costs is not a witness, he is a person standing near a thing.* He has attached a condition to the seat he offered and refused to name it, and asked for that to be entered.
12. **THE FIFTEEN-YEAR AND NINE-YEAR SPLIT IS UNCHANGED.** The lot book, the fourth line and the fifth line are fifteen years old; the table, the rail, the wall, the shop, the road keeper and the boy's page are nine. Sela Pike's bridge charter is sixteen and the boy is seventeen. **The clause has no stated age and its holder is sixty-one.**
13. **THE FOLLOW-ON MEASUREMENT FIGURES ARE THE REPAIRED ONES: 37,033 WORDS OVER TEN FILES, MEAN 3,703.3, MINIMUM 2,912, MAXIMUM 4,810. ZERO LADDER FAILURES. ZERO DUPLICATED PARAGRAPHS OF EIGHT WORDS OR MORE, WITHIN A FILE AND ACROSS THE TEN BOTH. ONE DUPLICATED SENTENCE AND IT IS A CARRIER. 421 CROSS-FILE TWELVE-WORD RUNS, AGAINST 445 IN BLOCK 0001 AND 495 IN BLOCK 0002. NO WEEKDAY, NO DATE OF RECORD, NO FOURTH-WALL BREAK, NO UNCLOSED SPEECH. THE BANNED SUBSTRING `have worked out that` IS AT ZERO. NINETY INSTANCES OF `about nine` AGAINST EIGHTY-THREE IN BLOCK 0002, AND ZERO OF THEM ARE THE PAUSE-LENGTH HEDGE THAT BLOCK 0002 DROVE TO ZERO.**
14. **THE FOUR FIGURES THAT ARE NOT ON A LADDER AND THAT A REVIEW OF THIS BLOCK FOUND PRINTED WRONG. Adrian's own tenure rises one a morning and is not `intercept + c`. It is eighty on the fifty-ninth morning and eighty-one on the sixtieth. Three places in Chapter 775 and one in Chapter 776 printed it as twenty and twenty-six, and all four are repaired. It is now eighty-six on Chapter 781 and ninety-five on Chapter 790, and the hundred is on Chapter 795, which is outside Block 0004. The other three figures that are not a ladder and were checked here are the second table at ninety-four, the near rail at forty-four, and the lane count at twenty-one, and all three are right. There is no other count in this yard that is not either a ladder row or on this list.**
15. **THREE BACK-REFERENCES WERE WRONG AND ARE REPAIRED, AND A SUCCESSOR SHOULD KNOW ALL THREE BECAUSE ALL THREE ARE THE KIND A FIGURE-BLOCK CHECK CANNOT SEE.** The woman of fifty-eight read out a name at the foot of that bank on the FIFTIETH morning, which is Chapter 766, and Chapter 780 dated it the fortieth. The woman who keeps a scale read a note out loud on the FIFTY-THIRD morning, which is Chapter 769, and Chapter 775 dated it nine mornings back, which is the fiftieth, when six is correct. And Chapter 780 had Adrian telling Iven Tallow on the fifty-ninth morning that the fault was his own sentence and his register, and Iven saying nothing at all: **no such exchange happened.** What did happen is that Adrian said *Then give it to a market* and Iven refused the sentence and not the man, at length. The chapter now says the true thing and the turn of the sentence, *both of those things were true and only one of them helped*, still lands.

# VOLUME 16, BLOCK 0003 — CONTINUITY CORRECTED BY THE SECOND REVIEW

> **THE SECOND REVIEW OF THIS BLOCK FOUND FIFTEEN CONTINUITY DEFECTS, ALL OF THEM A FIGURE OR A RELATIVE DAY IN A MOUTH, AND ALL OF THEM REPAIRED IN PLACE. THE LADDER CAME BACK CLEAN. THIS SECTION EXISTS SO THAT A SUCCESSOR DOES NOT RE-DERIVE THEM AND SO THAT THE CONVENTIONS THEY SETTLED ARE WRITTEN DOWN SOMEWHERE.**

1. **THE RELATIVE-DAY CONVENTION OF THIS VOLUME IS THE DIFFERENCE, NOT THE COUNT.** An event on the forty-ninth morning is *six mornings ago* on the fifty-fifth, *seven* on the fifty-sixth, and *fifteen* on the sixty-fourth — and the last of those is printed in the block itself, in Chapter 780. **A successor who counts inclusively will be one out on every back-reference in the volume, and the error will read as correct.**
2. **THE MORNING NUMBER IS `c + 34`.** Chapter 750 is the thirty-fourth morning, Chapter 751 the thirty-fifth, Chapter 771 the fifty-fifth, Chapter 780 the sixty-fourth. Chapter 761 is the forty-fifth morning and Chapter 764 the forty-eighth. **THE FIFTIETH MORNING IS CHAPTER 766 AND THE FIFTY-THIRD IS CHAPTER 769 AND THE FIFTY-FOURTH IS CHAPTER 770, AND EVERY EVENT IN THE PREVIOUS BLOCK'S RECORD IS DATED BY ONE OF THOSE FOUR CHAPTER NUMBERS.**
3. **TARIN KEST CAME DOWN THAT LANE ON THE FORTY-EIGHTH MORNING, CHAPTER 764, AND HAS BEEN IN THAT YARD THIRTEEN MORNINGS BY THE SIXTY-FIRST.** He is the only person in that yard who has been there thirteen mornings and it is the one figure in Chapter 777 that was printed wrong four times.
4. **ADRIAN'S OWN TENURE IS THE MORNING NUMBER PLUS TWENTY-ONE.** Seventy-six on the fifty-fifth, eighty on the fifty-ninth, eighty-one on the sixtieth, eighty-five on the sixty-fourth, and a hundred on the seventy-ninth, which is Chapter 795 and is outside Block 0004. **THE FIGURE A BOY OF SEVENTEEN COUNTED IN A YARD AND THE FIGURE A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX READ OFF NOTHING AT ALL ARE THE SAME LADDER AND THEY ARE NOT `intercept + c`, AND THE NUMBER OF MORNINGS A MAN HAS BEEN AT THE FOOT OF THAT BANK IS ALSO NOT `intercept + c` BUT IS EQUAL TO THE MORNING NUMBER ON ALL TEN MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE PREVIOUS BLOCK'S RECORD SAYS.**
5. **THE COUNT OF PEOPLE IN THAT YARD WHO ARE FINDABLE IS SIX BEFORE THE FIFTY-EIGHTH MORNING, NINE MORE ARE NAMED ON IT, AND ONE OF THOSE NINE CANNOT BE FOUND BY ANYBODY WHO COMES UP THAT LANE, WHICH MAKES ABOUT FOURTEEN BY THE SIXTY-THIRD, OF WHOM IVEN TALLOW SAYS HE IS THE FIFTEENTH.** Any successor who prints six after the fifty-eighth morning, or nine, is wrong against two of this block's own chapters.
6. **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE IS ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN AND HAS BEEN AT THAT TABLE FOR FIFTEEN YEARS, AND FIFTEEN IS SAID IN HIS OWN MOUTH IN CHAPTER 778.** He has been careful in a yard for nine of them, which is a different figure and is not a contradiction. **Nine and fifteen are both on this man and a successor must not let them merge.**
7. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR HAS BEEN ASKED TWO QUESTIONS IN HIS LIFE AND HAS ANSWERED BOTH**: by the man at the end of the second table on the forty-sixth morning, and by Tarin Kest on the sixty-first, which is the second time anybody has asked him anything in nine years. **The road keeper's question is *what is the run*, and the answer is the lamp, the cart, the doorway and the blanket, and the doorway is a thing he stands two hundred yards off to look at.**
8. **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX'S MORNINGS OFF THAT WALL ARE FIVE HUNDRED AND TWO ON THE SIXTY-FIRST, FIVE HUNDRED AND THREE ON THE SIXTY-SECOND, FIVE HUNDRED AND FOUR ON THE SIXTY-THIRD AND FIVE HUNDRED AND FIVE ON THE SIXTY-FOURTH, AND HIS READING OF THEM IS TWICE A MORNING, WHICH IS NOT NINE YEARS AND IS NOT ANY OTHER SPAN A SPEECH MAY GIVE IT.** Nine years is the age of the wall and of the man at the end of the second table's unfindability and of the book. It is not the age of a figure.
9. **IVEN TALLOW HAS BEEN ON THOSE STONES ON EVERY AFTERNOON SINCE HE ARRIVED.** He came up the lane at about half past two on the fifty-sixth morning and sat down that afternoon, and Chapter 780 calls it the ninth afternoon, and it is. **Three chapters printed it as the third, the fourth and the fifth afternoon running, and one chapter left him out of the afternoon entirely.**
10. **THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE IS FIVE AND UNPAID AND WAS NOT PAID BY ANYTHING IN THIS BLOCK.** A page with a stone on it is not a payment. A rule about who may be entered is not a payment. A refusal entered in a clerk's own book is not a payment. A seat offered for one reading is not a payment. **THE LIST WAS PRINTED TWICE IN THIS DISTRICT AND NOT A THIRD TIME.**
11. **THE COLUMN FOR WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD STILL HAS ONE NAME IN IT AND HAD NONE.** The boy of seventeen said out loud on the sixty-second morning that there are three people in that yard who have read a thing out loud in the last three weeks and one of them is on a page. **THE FOURTH IS ADRIAN, WHO READ FOUR LINES OUT LOUD HIMSELF ON THE SIXTY-FOURTH MORNING AND WHOM THE CLERK REFUSED TO ENTER ON THE SPOT BY SAYING *I AM NOT ENTERING THE FACT THAT YOU READ IT OUT LOUD EITHER*. THERE IS NO DISTRICT INSIDE THE THREE. THIS ITEM PREVIOUSLY SAID FOUR PEOPLE HAD READ AND THAT THE BOY HAD WORKED OUT THAT *THREE OF THEM ARE IN THIS DISTRICT*, WHICH IS NOT WHAT CHAPTER 778 SAYS AND WHICH THE THIRD REVIEW OF THIS BLOCK FOUND; THE DISTRICTS INSIDE THE THREE ARE AN INVENTED DISTINCTION AND ARE CUT.** **No second name has been entered and the clerk has refused one twice in public, and the rule that would let a second name in is a rule about who may be entered which the man of fifty-six has asked for and which nobody in that district can write.**
12. **THE FOLLOW-ON MEASUREMENT FIGURES ARE THE POST-SECOND-REVIEW ONES: 37,138 WORDS OVER TEN FILES, MEAN 3,713.8, MINIMUM 2,911, MAXIMUM 4,809. ZERO LADDER FAILURES, MEASURED CELL BY CELL IN EACH ROW'S OWN CARRIER PHRASE AND INSIDE ITS OWN SENTENCE BOUNDARY. ROW 12 CONSTANT ON TEN OF TEN. ROW 18 ABSENT ON TEN OF TEN AND NOT INVENTED. ROWS 11 AND 12 IN SEPARATE SENTENCES ON TEN OF TEN. EVERY STATIC-COUNT CARRIER PRESENT ON TEN OF TEN. ZERO DUPLICATED PARAGRAPHS OF EIGHT WORDS OR MORE, WITHIN A FILE AND ACROSS THE TEN BOTH. TWO DUPLICATED SENTENCES AND BOTH ARE CARRIER PHRASES OF THE STATIC COUNTS. NO WEEKDAY, NO DATE OF RECORD, NO FOURTH-WALL BREAK, EVEN QUOTE COUNT ON ALL TEN. EIGHTY-FIVE INSTANCES OF `about nine` COUNTED AS `about nine` NOT FOLLOWED BY `teen`, `hundred` OR `thousand`.** The shared-twelve-word-run figure is withdrawn rather than corrected.
13. **THE AGE OF THAT YARD, MEANING HOW LONG THE PEOPLE IN IT HAVE BEEN LEARNING WHAT IT IS FOR, IS ELEVEN MORNINGS, AND THE UNIT IS MORNINGS AND NOT DAYS.** It is stated that way about twenty times across the block, by the boy, by the man of fifty-six, by Mara Quill, by Tarin Kest and by the man at the end of the second table, and it does not move across the ten mornings. **A PASS OF THIS BLOCK HAD IT AT FOUR VALUES — *FOURTEEN DAYS* ON THE FIFTY-EIGHTH, *ABOUT ELEVEN DAYS* ON THE SIXTIETH, *A FORTNIGHT* ALSO ON THE SIXTIETH, AND *THIRTEEN MORNINGS* IN CHAPTER 777 — AND CHAPTER 777 PRINTED *ELEVEN DAYS* AND *THIRTEEN MORNINGS* IN THE SAME CHAPTER. ALL OF THE DAY FORMS ARE NOW MORNINGS. THE THIRTEEN MORNINGS IN CHAPTER 777 ARE NOT THIS FIGURE AND MUST NOT BE MADE INTO IT: THIRTEEN IS TARIN KEST'S OWN SPAN AND IT IS CORRECT, BECAUSE HE CAME DOWN THAT LANE ON THE FORTY-EIGHTH MORNING.** A successor who meets *eleven days* or *a fortnight* naming that yard's age is meeting an unattributable unit and should cut it rather than renumber it.
14. **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX HAS NO SPAN AND MAY NOT BE GIVEN ONE, INCLUDING A NEGATIVE ONE.** Item 8 settles his mornings at five hundred and two on the sixty-first and five hundred and five on the sixty-fourth, read twice a morning. **A PASS OF THIS BLOCK REPAIRED *A THING HE HAS NOT DONE IN NINE YEARS* IN THE LINE THAT SAID HE HAD BEEN DOING IT FOR NINE YEARS, AND LEFT ITS EXACT TWIN ONE LINE ABOVE.** He now stands still afterwards and says one more sentence, and the thing he did is one he does not remember doing. **NO SPEECH, FIGURE BLOCK OR NARRATIVE PASSAGE MAY GIVE THAT MAN A SPAN IN YEARS, DAYS, MONTHS OR FORTNIGHTS.**
15. **THE CLERK'S ENTRY OF THE SIXTIETH MORNING WAS NOT ON THE WALL BEFORE THE BOY PUT IT THERE.** She entered it in the lot book and read it out in that yard at about four. The boy of seventeen copied it onto the wall in chalk at about five, in her words and in her writing, and read it out loud while he was doing it, because there was nowhere else in that yard to put a thing he had not been asked to enter; the turner read it twice and went on up the hill. **A PASS OF THIS BLOCK HAD THE ENTRY ALREADY ON THE WALL IN A CLERK'S HAND BEFORE HE ARRIVED, AND ALSO SLIPPED FROM *THAT NIGHT* TO *THAT MORNING* IN THE SAME SENTENCE, AND THE RECORD REPEATED THE HALF-FAULT FOR TWO BLOCKS.** Chapter 778's line about the wall on the sixty-second morning is true against the repaired Chapter 776 and is unchanged.

HE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THE DISTRICT WHO HAD BEEN ASKED FOR THAT COLUMN, AND HE NEVER ASKED FOR HIMSELF, AND HE IS NOT IN IT.** He did not argue, said out loud that he knew it would not go his way, and said he asked for it because what he asked for was not the column. **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE TOLD HIM TO HIS FACE THAT IT IS NOT THE FRIGHT AND NOT AN ACCIDENT AND THAT WANTING HAS NOT BEEN ONE OF THE THINGS ON OFFER AT THAT WALL. The ninth line is the one about a term and fresh consent and the persons a market represents. **THE COUNT OF THINGS THIS DISTRICT HAS MADE DID NOT MOVE, A CONVERSATION IS NOT A THING FOUR PEOPLE MADE, AND THE CLERK COULD NOT ENTER IT.** The page he promised has not come and will not come and he said why: a page in a clerk's own hand is the only evidence worth anything and he cannot write in her hand; the carrier refused it on the fifty-eighth morning and was asked again on the sixty-fourth and said yes and carried a page in his cart; and the woman with a key must not carry it, because a page the Council produces will be heard as a Council document. **THE LOCATION OF THE CLAUSE IS STILL NOT PRINTED TO ANYBODY WHO CAN BE ASKED.**

He asked the woman with a key to enter his own refusal, and the clerk refused to enter it, and the boy's own page is now the safest thing he owns.

IT IS THE THIRD PASS, NOT THE FIRST TWO, THAT FOUND THE CLASS OF DEFECT THIS BLOCK ACTUALLY HAD, WHICH IS A RECORD THAT STATES FIGURES ITS OWN PROSE DOES NOT SUPPORT. EVERY FINDING WAS RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE CHAPTERS BEFORE ANY WORD WAS CHANGED. NOT ONE BLOCKING DEFECT IN ANY OF THE THREE PASSES WAS A LADDER CELL.**

1. **THE MORNING NUMBER IS `c + 34` AND THE MORNING NUMBER PLUS TWENTY-ONE IS ADRIAN'S OWN TENURE.** That is eighty-six on the sixty-fifth morning and ninety-five on the seventy-fourth, and it is printed on **three** mornings only — Chapter 782, Chapter 787 and Chapter 790. **THE PREVIOUS LINE IN THIS FILE SAID SIX AND A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT BUILD ON IT.**
2. **THE MAN WHO KEEPS A TALLY HAS TWO TIMES AND THEY ARE FIXED BY `chapter-0777.md`: HE SAYS A COUNT AT A QUARTER TO EIGHT AND HE ENTERS IT AT A QUARTER PAST NINE, AND THOSE TWO ARE AN HOUR AND A HALF APART.** `chapter-0782.md` and `chapter-0783.md` had them swapped and called the gap *up to an hour and a half*, which is also false of a quarter to eight and ten o'clock, since that pair is two hours and a quarter. **All three now carry the canon pair. On the seventy-third morning the order is reversed on purpose and the apparatus says so in words instead of saying *anyway*.**
3. **A SPAN IN YEARS MAY BE GIVEN TO THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR AND ONLY TO HIM.** He says he has been on a page for about nine years in Chapter 786, which is what the same chapter twice says, and the earlier *about nine mornings* in the same speech was wrong and is repaired.
4. **THE TWO MEN WHO ARE NOT THE SAME PERSON.** The man of fifty-six **asked for** the rule about who may be entered. **The man at the end of the second table made it**, on the sixty-eighth morning, out loud, before anybody was refused, and the rule's first victim is the man who asked for it. **A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT MERGE THEM, AND ONE HEADING IN THE BLOCK RECORD DID AND HAS BEEN CORRECTED.**
5. **THE PAGE WITH NINE NAMES ON IT IS NOT ON THE SECOND TABLE.** It went on those boards with a stone on the corner of it at about half past four on the fifty-eighth morning. `chapter-0780.md:127`, the sixty-fourth morning, records the second table bare, the stone lying face up, the hollow an inch deep and nothing at either end. It is sixteen mornings old, it is on none of the ten mornings of this block, and **its whereabouts are unstated.** The block record and the next phase's brief both described it as still lying there and both have been corrected.
6. **THE ROW ON THE BOY'S OWN SHEET IS ABOUT TWO YEARS OLD, NOT FOUR.** `chapter-0778.md:47` fixes it and the man at the end of the second table says two in his own mouth in Chapter 785. The boy overstated it by a factor of two and now says two.
7. **THE GATEPOST FIGURE IS GIVEN NO HISTORY, NO AUDIENCE AND NO RANKING ON ANY OF THE TEN MORNINGS.** Three figure blocks asserted what *nobody in this basin has ever* done, which is a claim about every room in the basin made by an apparatus that has seen one yard; all three are narrowed to that yard. Both were the same defect. The paragraph was cut and the chapter now ends on a boy's pencil.
9. **THE SIX DUPLICATED-SENTENCE FIGURES AND THE TRUE ONE.** After the third pass there are **seven** duplicated sentences of ten words or more across the ten files and **none of them is prose.** Nine of the previous sixteen stopped being duplicates because the third pass cut a figure block restating a count it had already printed, and one was a real prose tic — *and about four people said as sent under their breath*, verbatim in three chapters — now varied in two of the three. **Zero duplicated paragraphs of eight words or more, within a file and across the ten, before and after.**
10. **NO FIGURE BLOCK MAY ASSERT A FACT NO SCENE IN ITS CHAPTER ESTABLISHES, AND IT HAS NOW FAILED THAT TEST IN FIVE CONSECUTIVE BLOCKS.** Six further instances in this block, all cut or reduced to what the chapter has: a speech the chapter does not contain, a nine-word utterance with a duration attached to it, four people who used to say two unestablished things, an eleven-morning history of a person standing at the top of a wall, an aggregate nobody measured, and four figure blocks claiming to know what a whole basin had never done.

ZERO LADDER FAILURES. THE ROW 12 CONSTANT IS FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN ON TEN OF TEN AND APPEARS EXACTLY ONCE IN EACH. EVERY STATIC-COUNT CARRIER IS PRESENT ON TEN OF TEN. 139 SCENE DIVIDERS, MEAN 13.9, MINIMUM 12, MAXIMUM 17.** **A REVIEWER OF THE THIRD PASS REPORTED 140 DIVIDERS AND 140 IS WRONG; CHAPTER 784 HAS FIFTEEN AND THE TOTAL IS 139, WHICH IS WHAT THE BLOCK RECORD SAID.**
12. **THE CONDITION ON THE SEAT IS STILL UNNAMED, ADRIAN HAS STILL NOT SAID YES OR NO, THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE IS STILL FIVE AND UNPAID, THE COLUMN STILL HAS ONE NAME IN IT THAT DOES NOT SATISFY THE RULE THAT GOVERNS IT, AND THE ANSWER TO THE QUESTION THAT WENT UP THAT LANE HAS STILL NOT CAME DOWN IT.** None of the twenty-two repairs touched any of these, and none of them may be resolved by a successor who has not read this file.

## 27. VOLUME 19, BLOCK 0002 — CHAPTERS 901 TO 910. THE FIGURES AND THE RULES THAT CARRY INTO BLOCK 0003

> **Appended 2026-10-01 by the review fix of Volume 19 Block 0002. It wrote no chapter, altered no chapter, and restarted no batch. Every figure here is measured against the ten chapter files of `chapters/volume-19/` and the chapters are canon against this section.**

**WHAT MOVED IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS ONE FIGURE.** The page at the back of that lot book is at **thirty-seven** lines on all ten mornings and **took no line on any of them.** The inherited measure is that on a morning somebody in that yard asks the man of fifty-six a question out loud, one line goes on that page, the line carries the morning and not the question and not the answer, and the clerk reads the morning out loud before she writes it. **No morning of this block asks the man of fifty-six anything out loud, including the one that asks a clerk to read something, and so the page is at thirty-seven on all ten.** The figure block may not print that the asking has been stopped, withdrawn, narrowed or continued, and may not print the number of mornings without an asking, and may not print a figure for the count of moments on which somebody said a reason out loud before the page began to take them, which has no intercept.

**WHAT DID NOT MOVE AND IS NOT PAID BY ANY OF IT.** The lane count is **thirty** on all ten mornings. The figure on the sheet at that gatepost is **four hundred and eleven** on all ten and **its size of error is uncomputed, unexplanation and unpaid on every one of them.** The count of counted months is **seven**. This district has made **fourteen** things and the fourteen did not move. The fifth of the five is **five** and is unpaid on every morning of both blocks, and a form lying under a clean page for six mornings is not a payment of it. The toll is **ninety-eight** and is spent and may not be raised or lowered or paid twice. The ground at the foot of that low wall has been public lot since the afternoon of the one hundred and seventeenth morning, and a public lot is not a payment of the fifth of the five. The bid was appended at four hundred and fifty days, was not run, is not a lot and will not be. The heading over the one name in that column still says *whoever* and the name does not satisfy the rule. The man of thirty-eight has no bed, no wage and no office, has asked for none of the three, and has not been told he is a good man.

**THE LADDER AT `c = 20`, WHICH IS CHAPTER 910 AND IS THE INHERITANCE FOR BLOCK 0003.** Every cell is the declared intercept plus `c` and **every cell in every row added one across this block and the intercept was not fixed for any of them.** Row 1 **808**, row 2 **1124**, row 3 **838**, row 4 **799**, row 5 **struck** and printing four hundred and fifty in the past tense once per file, row 6 **691**, row 7 **513**, row 8 **518**, row 9 **588**, row 10 **527**, row 11 **649**, row 12 **411 and a constant whose size of error may not be computed**, row 13 the **549th**, row 14 **548**, row 15 **467**, row 16 **453**, row 17 the **635th**, row 18 **gone and not invented**, row 19 **109**. The declared direction split is ten rows before their carrier — 4, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 13, 15, 16 and 17 — and seven after — 1, 2, 3, 6, 12, 14 and 19. **Rows 11 and 12 are never in one sentence on any of the ten mornings and were checked for it.** The three exact hundreds are row 1 and row 9 on chapter 902 and row 19 on chapter 901, all spelled with no *and*, and row 6 is at *six hundred and ninety* on chapter 909 with its *and*.

**AND THE THING A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT SETTLE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE BLOCK.** At `c = 20` a clerk read the page at the back of that lot book **to herself** and said one word out loud, and **the word was three.** A word is a figure and a figure is a morning and a morning is not an answer. **No chapter may explain why she read it to herself, may print which three, may print how many of the thirty-seven are in her hand, may print that the answer is a count of hands, may call it an answer to anything, and may not put it in a figure block as a figure.** And no chapter may ever state, in either direction, **whether the three the clerk said at `c = 20` and the three lines nobody in this district heard read are the same three.** Block 0002 measured the strings *same three*, *were the same*, *count of hands*, *in her hand* and *because she read* across its ten mornings: four of the five are at **zero** on all ten and *were the same* is at **one**, on `chapter-0901.md`, where the sentence is about the same about four people noticing a figure on two mornings and is **not** a figure about the three in either direction. **A string that can be used for both things must be measured in both places, and a zero that is not true is worse than a one.**

## 28. VOLUME 20, BLOCK 0001 — CHAPTERS 931 TO 940 — CONTINUITY

**THE MEASURED POSITION AT THE END OF THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE ONLY FIGURE HERE THAT A WRITER MAY BUILD ON WITHOUT MEASURING IT AGAIN. EVERYTHING ELSE IS IN `state/volume-20-batch-0001-summary.md`, WHICH IS THE RECORD OF THIS BLOCK.**
